If we get λ from dual, we can update f using **Frank-Wolfe**: direction g = f * exp(-η Fλ f?) Wait Fλ is a vector h_i = (λ F)_i = Σ_k λ_k f_{k-i}. By minimax theorem for linear functions, this equals min_g max_λ λ^T F g = min_{g simplex} max_{λ simplex} Σ_i g_i h_i(λ). The LP solution g* is a minimizer of max over λ. In zero-sum game with payoff matrix F^T (λ rows? g columns), optimal g is a mixed strategy minimizing maximum expected payoff; optimal λ maximizing minimum. The payoff is bilinear in λ,g. So indeed the linearized problem is a zero-sum matrix game with matrix F (lags vs positions). We can solve the game via **matrix game iteration** (multiplicative weights) without LP: alternate best responses or mirror descent. This can be much faster and can also provide gradient for updating f? Interesting.

For current f, solve game: row player λ over lags, column g over positions, payoff F_{k,i}=f_{k-i} (zero if invalid). We want row maximize, column minimize? Column payoff = λ^T F g, row wants max, column min. Value μ. The optimal column g* is exactly the SCA direction. We can approximate g*, λ* via multiplicative weights for the matrix F (size ~1535x768, nonnegative, sparse). This avoids LP solves and can be fast (matrix-vector O(nnz) per iteration). Since F has convolution structure, matrix-vector products fast via FFT or slice loops? But λ vector length 1535; Fλ = weighted cross-correlation can be computed via FFT (linear convolution) O(L log L). Similarly F^T λ? Actually F^T λ = h. F g = f*g (FFW). So game iteration:
- Initialize λ uniform or best response to f (peak weights), g uniform or f.
- Iterate: λ ← softmax(η F g) (row maximizing expected payoff against g), g ← softmax(-η F^T λ) (column minimizing expected payoff against λ).
This is multiplicative weights for matrix game. After T iterations, g approximates SCA direction, λ approximates dual weights. Cost per iter convolutions: F g is convolution f*g (FFT), F^T λ is convolution? (λ* f?) can compute. Very fast (~ms for n=768). We can run thousands of iterations. Then use g as SCA update.

Implement general linear convolution via FFT:
```
def lin_convolve(u,v):
    n=len(u); m=len(v); size=n+m-1; fft_size=1<<(size-1).bit_length(); U=np.fft.rfft(u,fft_size); V=np.fft.rfft(v,fft_size); return np.fft.irfft(U*V,fft_size)[:size]
```
For performance, when one operand repeated, we can use `convolve_with_cache(f_cache, x)` but cache includes fft size depending on x length. g length n, lam_rev length L; different sizes? f*g size 2n-1, f*lam_rev size n+L-1=3n-2. Could compute separately. We'll not over-optimize unless needed.

But inside game inner, f fixed, g length n, lam length L. We can compute:
- size_g = next_pow2(2n-1). A_g = rfft(f, size_g)
- size_l = next_pow2(3n-2). A_l = rfft(f, size_l)
Then each iteration: B_g=rfft(g,size_g); fg=irfft(A_g*B_g,size_g)[:2n-1]. B_l=rfft(lam_rev,size_l); FtL=irfft(A_l*B_l,size_l)[L-1 : L-n : -1]. Note lam is length L. This is efficient. We'll implement in top-level `game_step_f_fixed(f, g, lam, Tg, cache)`? But no closures; can define a helper `convolve_ff_cache` maybe. Simpler: within function, maintain caches and compute directly with numpy.

Need stable softmax for large arrays:
```
def softmax(x, axis=-1):
    xmax=np.max(x)
    e=np.exp(x-xmax)
    s=e.sum()
    if s==0 or not finite: return uniform
    return e/s
```
For row maximizing, use `softmax(fg / Tg)`. For column minimizing, use `softmax(-FtL / Tg)` (or `softmax((FtL - min)/Tg)` reversed). Values small; no overflow if divide.

We also need `fast_convolve` for actual objective. Can use `lin_convolve(a,a)` but same length. Use rfft with size 2n-1 rounded.

Line search helper:
```
def line_search_blend(f, g, curr_M, alphas=None):
    best_a=f; best_M=curr_M
    for alpha in alphas: 
       a=(1-alpha)*f+alpha*g
       a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<=0 continue; a/=s
       M=conv_max(a)
       if M<best_M: best...
    return best_a,best_M
```

Let's write top-level helper functions:

def normalize_pos(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=1e-15: return np.ones(len(a))/len(a)
    return a/s
```

n=len(a); size=2*n-1; fft=1<<(size-1).bit_length(); A=np.fft.rfft(a,fft); out=np.fft.irfft(A*A, fft)[:size]; return out
```

`softmax_weights(x, temperature=None, scale=1.0)`:

if temperature is None or temperature<=0: return np.argmax one?

For active softmax with M subtract: `active_weights(conv, tau)`:
```
w=np.exp((conv-conv.max())/tau); s=w.sum(); if s<=0: w=0; w[np.argmax(conv)]=1; else w/=s
```
This ensures weights sum1 and focus near max.

Implement `lse_mirror_optimize` with time. Let's make it more sophisticated:
```
def lse_mirror_optimize(a_init, time_limit, tau_frac=0.05, move_scale=0.3, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_pos(a_init); n=len(a)
    conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=conv.max(); best=₁a.copy(); best_M=M
    tau = max(1e-12, tau_frac*M)
    ms = move_scale
    eta_base = ms
    t0=time.monotonic(); it=0; no_improve=0
    while time -t0 < time_limit:
       it+=1
       p=soft_active_weights(conv, tau)
       h=weighted_conv_lambda_f(p,a)
       h_max=h.max()
       if h_max<1e-30: break
       eta=ms/h_max
       a_raw = a * np.exp(-eta*h)
       # add small uniform to avoid zeros
       a_new = a_raw + 1e-12
       a_new /= a_new.sum()
       # line search alphas [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.8,1.0]
       a_best=M; cand=M
       for alpha in [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.8,1.0]:
           b=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*a_new; b=np.maximum(b,0); b/=b.sum()
           cb=fast_conv_same(b); mb=cb.max()
           if mb < a_best: a_best=mb; best_b=b; best_cb=cb
       if a_best < M - 1e-14:
           a=best_b; conv=best_cb; M=a_best
           if M < best_M: best_M=M; best= a.copy(); no_improve=0
           else no_improve=1
           # adapt: if improved, maybe reduce tau slightly, increase ms slightly
           tau *=0.95; ms = min(1.0, ms*1.05)
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           # adapt: increase tau (smoother), reduce move
           tau *=1.1; ms = max(0.05, ms*0.9)
           if no_improve > 50:
               # perturb best
               eps = 0.002 + 0.004*(no_improve//50 %3)
               a=normalize_pos(best + rng.standard_normal(n)*eps) # need rng passed? pass seed/it
               conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=conv.max(); tau=max(1e-12, 0.1*M); ms=0.3; no_improve=0
    return best,best_M
```
Need `rng` inside; use `np.random.default_rng((hash?) )`. To be deterministic, use `seed` parameter. For restarts, create rng = default_rng(seed + it). But creating rng each time okay. Or pass rng. We can define `perturb_simplex(base, scale, seed)` top-level.

def game_sca_optimize(a_init, time_limit, temp_frac=0.2, inner_block=20, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_pos(a_init)
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=conv.max(); best=a.copy(); best_M=M
    # caches for f=a:
    size_g=next_pow2(2*n-1); size_l=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    Af_g=np.fft.rfft(a,size_g); Af_l=np.fft.rfft(a,size_l)
    g=a.copy(); g_avg=a.copy()
    lam=soft_active_weights(conv, temp_frac*M); lam_avg=lam.copy()
    T=max(1e-15, temp_frac*M)
    t0=time.monotonic(); it=0; no_improve=0; outer=0
    while time<...:
       # inner iterations
       for _ in range(inner_block):
           # fg = conv(a,g)
           Gg=np.fft.rfft(g,size_g); fg=irfft(Af_g*Gg)[:L]
           # row update lam
           row = softmax(fg / T)  # maximizing
           # FtL = weighted_corr(a, lam) using Af_l and lam_rev
           lamr=lam[::-1]; Lg=np.fft.rfft(lamr,size_l); FtL=irfft(Af_l*Lg)[:size_l]; h=FtL[L-1:L-1-n:-1]
           # col update g
           col = softmax(-h / T)
           g = col
           lam = row
           # average
           g_avg = (1-beta)*g_avg + beta*col
           lam_avg = (1-beta)*lam_avg + beta*row
       g_use=normalize_pos(g_avg)
       # linearized value estimate: max conv(a, g_use)
       val = fast_conv_same? No, conv(a,g_use).max maybe using cache.
       # line search
       newa,newM=blend_line_search(a,g_use,M, alphas)
       if newM < M - 1e-14:
           a=newa; M=newM; conv=fast_conv_same(a)
           if M<best_M: best=a.copy(); best_M=M; no_improve=0; print
           # update caches for new a
           Af_g=...; Af_l=...
           # adjust temp: maybe T*0.95 (sharpen)
           T = max(1e-15, 0.5*T, 0.1*?); # careful
           temp_frac = max(0.05, temp_frac*0.95)
           T = temp_frac*M
           # reset averages maybe
           g=a.copy(); g_avg=a.copy(); lam=soft_active_weights(conv,T); lam_avg=lam.copy()
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           # adapt: increase temp, different alphas
           temp_frac = min(2.0, temp_frac*1.1)
           T = temp_frac*M
           if no_improve > 30:
              # perturb a from best
              eps=0.003+...
              a=normalize_pos(best + rng.standard_normal(n)*eps)
              M=convmax; if M<best_M? no; update best maybe
              Af... reset
              temp_frac=0.2; T=temp_frac*M
              no_improve=0
    return best,best_M
```
Need `softmax` stable with input `fg/T`. fg values ~M, T=0.2M => exponent max 5, fine. For col, -h/T: h values maybe around M? Since h=F^Tλ, max maybe ~M, min lower. Exponents negative up to -5, fine. If T too small, underflow okay with softmax subtract max. For col, to minimize, use `softmax(-h/T)`, which puts weight on min h. Good.

One subtlety: In matrix game, row payoff Fg and column payoff F^Tλ. But row maximizing should update λ ∝ λ exp(η Fg). Starting uniform; using softmax(Fg/T) from scratch each iteration is equivalent best response (η→∞). Standard multiplicative with memory would multiply; but using softmax from scratch okay if we want active set. Maybe better to keep memory: `log_lambda += γ fg/T; norm`. But softmax from scratch may oscillate. We use averaging. Good.

Should we use `lam = softmax(fg / T)` or `lam = softmax((fg - fg.max()) / T)` same. For row maximizing, if all fg similar, uniform. For column, `softmax(-h/T)` puts mass on minimum h. Standard column minimization best response at min. Good.

At equilibrium, g minimizes max fg, λ maximizes min h. The value T? Not directly.

Line search: We should maybe try `g` from game and also **dual** h? Could update a via multiplicative based on h: a_new = a / h? But g is enough.

Also we can use the actual convolution `conv` to reset λ when no progress: `lam = soft_active_weights(conv, T)` (peaks of actual). The game λ is for linearized f*g, not actual f*f. If f and g diverge, λ might not correspond to actual peaks. We can blend lam_avg with actual peak weights.

Maybe simpler: Use game only to generate g, but λ should also be influenced by actual peaks? The linearized direction depends on f; the game solves min_g max_k (f*g)_k. This is the best SCA direction. No need actual peaks except to reset if stuck. Good.

We need update f caches after f changes. `Af_l` for size_l depends on a only. Good.

Potential issue: The matrix F for SCA linearization uses f rows? Actually (f*g)_k = Σ_i f_{k-i} g_i. Matrix entry F_{k,i}=f_{k-i}. This matrix is not symmetric; row player lags, column positions. The minimax value gives g. Yes.

We also could solve the **actual** SCA by minmax LP more accurately. The game solver approximates. To avoid approximation error, we can run LP for final polish.

def build_conv_structure(n):
    L=2*n-1
    rows=[]; cols=[]
    for k in range(L):
        start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)
        if start<=end:
            cols.extend(range(start,end+1)); rows.extend([k]*(end-start+1))
    return np.array(rows,dtype=np.int32), np.array(cols,dtype np.int32)
```

```
def sca_lp_step(f, row_idx, col_idx):
    n=len(f); L=2*n-1
    vals=f[row_idx - col_idx]
    # A_ub for min t:
    data=np.concatenate([vals, -np.ones(L)])
    r=np.concatenate([row_idx, row_idx])
    c=np.concatenate([col_idx, np.arange(L, dtype=np.int32)])
    A_ub=csr_matrix((data.astype(np.float64),(r,c)), shape=(L,n+1))
    b_ub=np.zeros(L)
    q=np.zeros(n+1); q[-1]=1
    # A_eq for sum g=1
    A_eq=csr_matrix((np.ones(n), (np.arange(n), np.arange(n))), shape=(1,n+1))
    b_eq=np.ones(1)
    bounds=[(0,None)]*n + [(0,None)]
    res=linprog(c_q, A_ub=A_ub,b_ub=b_ub,A_eq=A_eq,b_eq=b_eq,bounds=bounds,method='highs',options={'presolve':True,'time_limit':?})
```
But options `time_limit` maybe available? Could set 5s. scipy linprog highs options include time_limit? I think HiGHS has time_limit. But if set, may fail. Use default.

Detailed implementations:

`normalize_nonneg(a, floor=0.0)`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a, floor=0.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    if floor>0:
        # add floor to all? floor absolute
        a=a+floor
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-300:
        l=len(a)
        return np.ones(l,dtype=np.float64)/l if l>0 else a
    return a/s
```
For floor small like 1e-12, adding to all makes sum increase; okay. If we want relative floor, `a = np.maximum(a, floor)`. But if floor absolute 1e-12, fine.

`lin_convolve(u,v)`:
```
def lin_convolve(u,v):
    u=np.asarray(u,float); v=np.asarray(v,float)
    n=len(u); m=len(v); size=n+m-1
    if size<=0: return np.array([])
    fft_size=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    U=np.fft.rfft(u, fft_size); V=np.fft.rfft(v, fft_size)
    out=np.fft.irfft(U*V, fft_size)[:size]
    out[np.abs(out)<1e-300]=0.0
    return out
```
Potential negative tiny due to FFT; for objective max, small negative irrelevant. Could `np.maximum(out,0)`? For weights maybe use as is; we can clip in scores: `out = np.maximum(out,0)`. Since convolution of nonnegative should be nonnegative, clip to remove negative numerical. Do that.

`fast_conv_same(a)=lin_convolve(a,a)`.

`score_direct(a)`:
```
s=a.sum(); if s<=... return inf
an=a/s; conv=fast_conv_same(an); return float(2*len(an)*conv.max())
```
If a already normalized, still sum.

`soft_active_weights(conv, tau)`:
```
if tau<=0 or tau<1e-300:
    w=np.zeros(len(conv)); w[np.argmax(conv)]=1; return w
z=(conv - conv.max())/tau
# after subtract max, z<=0; exp
z = z - z.max() # redundant max0
e=np.exp(z)
s=e.sum()
if not finite or s<=0: uniform
return e/s
```
If tau very small, e only max 1 and maybe ties 1; okay.

`uniform_weights(len)`.

`softmax(x)` general:
```
y=x - np.max(x)
e=np.exp(y); s=e.sum(); if s==0 or not finite: uniform; return e/s
```
If x contains NaN? handle.

`weighted_corr(lam, f)`:
```
L=len(lam); rev=lam[::-1]
conv=lin_convolve(f, rev)
# length len(f)+L-1
stop = L-1 - len(f)
return conv[L-1 : stop : -1]
```
Check if stop may be -1; okay. But if n large, conv length = n+L-1 =3n-2. Index L-1 within. Good.
`h` might have tiny negatives; clip? h used in exponent, should be nonnegative; clip `h=np.maximum(h,0)`? If lam, f nonnegative, h nonnegative. Numerical tiny neg, clip.

`blend_line_search(f,g,curr_M, alphas=None)`:
```
if alphas is None: alphas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0)
best_a=f.copy(); best_M=curr_M
for alpha in alphas:
   if alpha<=0: continue
   a=(1-alpha)*f+alpha*g
   a=np.maximum(a,0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=1e-300: continue
   a=a/s
   conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=conv.max()
   if M<best_M - 1e-15:
       best_M=M; best_a=a.copy()
return best_a,best_M,best_conv? return conv too to avoid recompute.
```
Return `best_a,best_M,best_conv`.

`interpolate_sequence(parent,new_n)` as above. If parent length 1, fill.

return normalize_nonneg(v)

`deterministic_candidates(n, seed)`:
Need not too heavy. Return list of arrays normalized. We can include parent interpolate outside.
Implementation:
```
def deterministic_candidates(n, seed=0):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    c=[]
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    idx=np.arange(n)
    def add(v): v=np.asarray(v,float); v=np.maximum(v,0); s=v.sum(); if s>0: c.append(v/s)
    add(np.ones(n))
    # powers on idx+1
    for p in [0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0]:
       add((idx+1.0)**p)
       add((n-idx)**p)
       add((idx+1.0)**p + (n-idx)**p*0.5)
    # polynomial on x
    for p in [0.5,1,2]:
       add(x**p)
       add((1-x)**p)
       add(x**p + 0.5*(1-x)**p)
    # sinusoids
    for m in [1,2,3,5,7]:
       for amp in [0.2,0.5,0.8,0.99]:
          add(1.0 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*x))
          add(1.0 + amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*x))
          add(1.0 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*x + 0.5))
    # heavy Dirichlet
    for alpha in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1,2,5,10]:
       add(rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,alpha)))
    # random clip Fourier
    for trial in range(5):
       k=rng.integers(1,8)
       amp=0.3+rng.random()*0.5
       v=1.0 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x + rng.random()*6.28) + 0.2*amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*(k+1)*x + rng.random()*6.28)
       add(v)
    # edge heavy
    d=np.minimum(x,1-x)+1e-6
    for p in [0.5,1,2]: add(1.0/d**p)
    # center heavy
    add(np.exp(-((x-0.5)*4)**2))
    # blocky
    for period in [2,3,5,8]:
       v = 1.0 + 0.5*np.cos(2*np.pi*idx/period)
       add(v)
    return c
```
Nested? no. Use helper `add` nested! That's a closure, not allowed. Need avoid nested functions. We'll implement without nested `add`: define local list and inline repeated code? Could be verbose. We can define a top-level `_add_candidate(c, v)` that modifies list. It's top-level. Use that. Or write a small loop:
```
def determinant_candidates(n, seed=0):
   c=[]; ...
   v=...; s=v.sum(); if s>0: c.append(v/s)
```
Duplicate but okay. Or top-level `append_normalized(c, v)`. Let's define top-level:
```
def append_normalized(c, v):
    v=np.asarray(v,dtype=float); v=np.maximum(v,0); s=v.sum()
    if s>1e-300 and np.isfinite(s): c.append(v/s)
    return c
```
Then in candidates use `append_normalized(c, ...)`. Good, no closure.

`try_candidate(state, a, label, force=False)`:
State: list [best_seq, best_actual, best_n, best_direct]
```
def try_candidate(state, a, label, force=False):
    if a is None: return inf
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.ndim!=1 or len(a)==0: return inf
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s): return inf
    an=a/s
    conv=fast_conv_same(an); M=conv.max(); direct=float(2*len(an)*M)
    if not np.isfinite(direct): return direct
    if force or direct < state[3] - 1e-12:
        try:
            actual=float(evaluate_sequence(an.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            actual=direct
        if np.isfinite(actual) and (force or direct < state[3] + 1e-9) and (actual < state[1] or state[0] is None):
            # If actual finite but direct better? We want update only if actual lower than state actual, but if actual evaluator different and direct not lower? force may not improve. Maybe update if actual < state[1]
            if state[0] is None or actual < state[1]:
                state[0]=an.copy(); state[1]=float(actual); state[2]=len(an); state[3]=direct
                print(f"NEW BEST [{label}] actual={state[1]:.8f} direct={direct:.8f} n={state[2]}")
        # If actual not lower but direct lower, maybe state[3] update? no need
    elif direct < state[3]:
        # direct improved but not evaluating; update direct threshold to allow future eval? but actual state not updated.
        state[3]=direct
    return direct
```
If state best actual is inf initially, need update first time even if actual not < inf? yes.
But if hidden evaluator actual is same direct, okay. If force True for optimizer result but actual not lower, no update.
At the end, we need return state[0] even if actual not finite? We'll ensure best set.
Maybe we should update state direct even if actual not evaluated: `if direct < state[3]: state[3]=direct`. But if state[0] None, state[3] inf.

Potential bug: If evaluate_sequence actual returns lower than state[1] but direct higher, our condition doesn't update because direct threshold. `force` only calls eval when direct improved. If hidden actual differs, might miss. To be safer, for initial candidates and after optimizers (force=True), we call evaluate and update if actual < state[1], regardless of direct. Condition:
```
if force or direct < state[3] - 1e-12:
   try actual=evaluate...
   if np.isfinite(actual) and (state[0] is None or actual < state[1]): update
if direct < state[3]: state[3]=direct
```
This way force updates actual even if direct not lower. Good.

But for many candidates, force=True would call evaluator many times. We'll use force=False for mass candidate generation, and only update actual when direct improves; final optimizers force=True. For candidate generation, if hidden differs, maybe miss a candidate with actual better but direct worse; unlikely.

`lse_mirror_optimize` details. Need use `soft_active_weights`, `weighted_corr`, `blend_line_search`.
```
def lse_mirror_optimize(a_init, time_limit, tau_frac=0.05, move_scale=0.3, seed=0, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a_init, floor=1e-12)
    n=len(a)
    conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max())
    best=a.copy(); best_M=M
    tau=max(1e-15, tau_frac*M)
    ms=move_scale
    t0=time.monotonic(); it=0; no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       it+=1
       p=soft_active_weights(conv, tau)
       h=weighted_corr(p, a)
       hmax=float(h.max())
       if hmax < 1e-300: break
       eta=ms/hmax
       a_raw=a * np.exp(-eta*h)
       a_new=normalize_nonneg(a_raw + 1e-12)
       newa,newM,newconv=blend_line_search(a, a_new, M)
       if newM < M - 1e-15:
           a=newa; M=newM; conv=newconv
           if M < best_M - 1e-15:
               best_M=M; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
               # adapt success
               tau=max(1e-15, tau*0.95)
               ms=min(1.0, ms*1.02)
           else:
               no_improve+=1
               tau=min(2.0*M+1e-15, tau*1.05)
               ms=max(0.05, ms*0.98)
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           tau=min(2.0*M+1e-15, tau*1.15)
           ms=max(0.05, ms*0.9)
           if no_improve > 40:
               scale=0.002 + 0.003*(1+ (no_improve//40) % 5)
               a=perturb_simplex(best, scale, seed + it)
               conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max())
               tau=max(1e-15, 0.1*M)
               ms=0.3
               no_improve=0
       if verbose and it%50 ==0: print(...)
    return best, best_M
```
Potential issue: `blend_line_search` returns best among alphas; if a_new is bad, it returns f,M. No improve.
`tau` cap `2.0*M`; if M changes, ensure tau not zero.
`move_scale` relative to hmax. If hmax small due p? hmax at least? okay.

Can we improve by using **actual gradient for LSE** in u-space? Maybe not.

`game_sca_optimize` implementation details robust:
```
def game_sca_optimize(a_init, time_limit, temp_frac=0.2, inner_block=20, beta=0.25, seed=0, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a_init, floor=1e-12)
    n=len(a); L=2*n-1
    conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max()); best=a.copy(); best_M=M
    size_g=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    size_l=1 << (3*n-3? actually n+L-1=3n-2 -1? size for linear conv f*lam_rev: len sum -1 = n + L -1 = n + (2n-1) -1 = 3n-2. next pow2 of 3n-2.
    size_l=1 << ((3*n-2)-1).bit_length()
    Af_g=np.fft.rfft(a, size_g)
    Af_l=np.fft.rfft(a, size_l)
    g=a.copy(); g_avg=a.copy()
    lam=soft_active_weights(conv, max(1e-15, temp_frac*M)); lam_avg=lam.copy()
    T=max(1e-15, temp_frac*M)
    t0=time.monotonic(); outer=0; no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       # inner iterations
       for _ in range(inner_block):
           Gg=np.fft.rfft(g, size_g)
           fg=np.fft.irfft(Af_g*Gg, size_g)[:L]
           row=softmax(fg / T)
           lamr=lam[::-1]
           Ll=np.fft.rfft(lamr, size_l)
           Rt=np.fft.irfft(Af_l*Ll, size_l)
           h=Rt[L-1 : L-1-n : -1]
           h=np.maximum(h,0)
           col=softmax(-h / T)
           g=col
           lam=row
           lam_avg = (1-beta)*lam_avg + beta*row
           g_avg = (1-beta)*g_avg + beta*col
       g_use=normalize_nonnorm? normalize_nonneg(g_avg, floor=0)
       # estimate val maybe not needed
       newa,newM,newconv=blend_line_search(a, g_use, M, alphas=(0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0))
       if newM < M - 1e-15:
           a=newa; M=newM; conv=newconv
           if M < best_M - 1e-15:
               best_M=M; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
               if verbose and best_M%? print
           # update caches
           Af_g=np.fft.rfft(a, size_g); Af_l=np.fft.rfft(a,size_l)
           # tighten temp
           temp_frac = max(0.05, temp_frac*0.95)
           T = max(1e-15, temp_frac*M)
           # reset averages? maybe keep but recenter to a/g
           g = a.copy(); g_avg = a.copy()
           lam = soft_active_weights(conv, T); lam_avg=lam.copy()
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           temp_frac = min(2.0, temp_frac*1.1)
           T = max(1e-15, temp_frac*M)
           if no_improve > outer? e.g., 30:
               scale=0.002 + 0.004*(1+(no_improve//30)%5)
               a = perturb_simplex(best, scale, seed+outer)
               conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max())
               if M < best_M: best_M=M; best=a.copy()
               Af_g=...; Af_l=...
               temp_frac=0.2; T=temp_frac*M
               g=a.copy(); g_avg=a.copy(); lam=soft_active_weights(conv,T); lam_avg=lam.copy()
               no_improve=0
       outer+=1
    return best,best_M
```
Check `size_l = next_pow2(3n-2)`: For n=1, 1? 3n-2=1, pow2=1; rfft size1 okay. But n likely >1.
`Rt[L-1 : L-1-n : -1]`: For n=1, L=1, stop=-1, returns length1. Good.
`softmax(fg/T)`: `fg` might have tiny negative; okay. If T too small, division overflow? fg ~1e-4, T~1e-6 => 100; exp okay. If T=1e-15, overflow; ensure T>=1e-12 maybe. Set `T=max(1e-12, temp_frac*M)`. M ~1e-3, T min 1e-12 okay. For large n M~1/1000=1e-3, T bigger.
`softmax(-h/T)`: h values can be up to ~1/n. okay.
Averaging beta maybe 0.2; after inner_block 20, g_avg moves reasonably.
When f updates small, resetting g=a may discard good direction. Maybe keep g_avg but renormalize relative? The direction g from linearized based on old a; if a changes small, still good. We can instead after update set `g = g_avg` (the direction) not a; `g_avg = convolve?` But the game needs g as column strategy; if f changes, the previous g is still a valid candidate. For next inner, row λ based on fg (new f * old g), column based. So keep `g = g_avg` (or previous g) rather than reset to a. Resetting to a might slow convergence. Let's set after update: `g = g_avg` (normalized), `lam = lam_avg`. But g_avg may have sum1. Good. Also `best` updated. If large update, maybe keep.
If no_improve and perturb, reset g=a.

Potential issue: The linearized minimax game value may be smaller than actual M if g chosen; line search may often improve even if best not? If blend doesn't improve actual M, no_improve. Could try **set f = g** with projection? blend includes alpha=1, so if g actual M lower, update. Good.

Can use the game's `row` λ to do actual subgradient update if blend fails: `a_new = a * exp(-eta*h)` (mirror) using h (dual cost). This might improve when g blend fails. Add in else block: compute h actual? We have h from last column update (F^T λ). Use as gradient: `a_mw = normalize_nonneg(a * exp(-0.1*h/hmax + 1e-12))`; line search. If improves, update. This leverages λ. We'll add.

In `game_sca_optimize`, after blend no improve, try MW with h from last iteration:
```
hmax=h.max(); eta=0.2/hmax; a_mw=normalize_nonneg(a*np.exp(-eta*h)+1e-12); newa2,newM2,_=blend_line_search(a,a_mw,M); if newM2<M: update...
```
This gives another move.

def sca_lp_optimize(a_init, time_limit, seed=0, max_elems=1_600_000, verbose=False):
   a=normalize_nonneg(a_init, floor=1e-12)
   n=len(a); if n*n > max_elems: return a, score_direct? (skip)
   L=2*n-1; row_idx,col_idx=build_conv_structure(n)
   t_rows=np.arange(L); t_cols=np.arange(L)
   best=a.copy(); conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=conv.max(); best_M=M
   M_lp=M
   t0=...; it=0; no_improve=0
   while ...:
      # Solve minmax LP maybe each iteration
      g = None
      try:
         g = sca_lp_minmax_step(a,row_idx,col_idx,t_rows,t_cols)
      except Exception: g=None
      if g is None:
         # fallback to LSE small for this step? break
         break
      g=normalize_nonneg(g, floor=0)
      newa,newM,newconv=blend_line_search(a,g,M)
      if newM < M - 1e-15:
          a=newa; M=newM; conv=newconv
          if M<best_M: best_M=M; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
          # M_lp maybe update to M*1.0? for minmax not needed
      else:
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve>10:
             a=perturb_simplex(best, 0.003, seed+it)
             conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=conv.max(); no_improve=0
      it+=1
   return best,best_M
```
`sca_lp_minmax_step` should return `res.x[:n]`. If HiGHS returns status? We'll catch.
Set `method='highs'`. Options maybe `{'presolve': True, 'primal_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9,...}` not necessary.
Potential issue: `linprog` may take more than time. We can't easily time-limit. To avoid hanging, we can check time before each solve and maybe if solve unexpectedly long? No. But HiGHS should be okay. For n=1024, A_ub 1M nnz, maybe 0.2s. If too slow, loop breaks after fewer.
`max_elems`: n*n. For n=1280 ->1.6M okay. For 1536 ->2.3M maybe slow; set max 1.8M.

Build structure: For n=1, L=1. counts? We'll implement loop:
```
def build_conv_structure(n):
    L=2*n-1
    rows=[]; cols=[]
    for k in range(L):
        start=max(0,k-n+1); end=min(n-1,k)
        count=end-start+1
        if count>0:
            rows.extend([k]*count)
            cols.extend(range(start,end+1))
    return np.array(rows,dtype=np.int32), np.array(cols,dtype=np.int32)
```
This uses Python loops; okay for n up to 1280 (1.6M appends maybe 0.2s). Could optimize by prealloc:
```
total=n*n
rows=np.empty(total,dtype=np.int32); cols=np.empty(total,dtype=np.int32)
idx=0
for k ...:
   c=end-start+1
   rows[idx:idx+c]=k
   cols[idx:idx+c]=np.arange(start,end+1,dtype=np.int32)
   idx+=c
```
This is faster. We'll implement:
```
total=n*n
rows=np.empty(total,dtype=np.int64); cols=...; idx=0
for k...
   rows[idx:idx+c]=k
   cols[idx:idx+c]=np.arange(start,end+1,dtype=np.int64)
   idx+=c
return rows,cols
```
Need int type for sparse; int32 enough if n<10000. Use np.int32. `rows[idx:idx+c] = k` works.

`sca_lp_minmax_step`:
```
def sca_lp_minmax_step(f,row_idx,col_idx,t_rows,t_cols):
    L=2*len(f)-1
    vals=f[row_idx - col_idx]
    data=np.concatenate((vals, -np.ones(L)))
    rr=np.concatenate((row_idx, t_rows))
    cc=np.concatenate((col_idx, t_cols))
    A_ub=sp.csr_matrix((data,(rr,cc)), shape=(L,len(f)+1))
    b_ub=np.zeros(L)
    c=np.zeros(len(f)+1); c[-1]=1
    A_eq=sp.csr_matrix((np.ones(len(f)), (np.arange(len(f)), np.arange(len(f)))), shape=(1,len(f)+1))
    b_eq=np.ones(1)
    bounds=[(0.0,None)]*len(f)+[(0.0,None)]
    res=opt.linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub,b_ub=b_ub,A_eq=A_eq,b_eq=b_eq,bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve':True})
    if res.success and res.x is not None:
        g=np.maximum(res.x[:len(f)],0); s=g.sum(); if s>0: return g/s
    return None
```
Could t be negative? constraints A g ≤ t; t bound 0,None. If feasible, t≥M? min nonnegative. Good.
If HiGHS not available? scipy has. Import `from scipy.optimize import linprog`.

Maybe LP with unbounded t? t has lower 0. Good.

Now, global propose_candidate orchestration.

Pseudo:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    start_time=time.monotonic()
    budget_s=max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline=start_time + budget_s - 30
    state=[None, float('inf'), 0, float('inf')]
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # load parent
    parent=None
    try:
       p=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
       if p.ndim==1 and p.size>0:
          p=np.clip(p,0,None)
          if np.isfinite(p).all() and p.sum()>1e-10:
             parent=normalize_nonneg(p, floor=0)
             try_candidate(state,parent,'parent',force=True)
    except Exception as e: pass
    # candidate generation
    n_set = []
    if parent is not None: n_set.append(len(parent))
    for n in [256,384,512,640,768,896,1024,1280,1536,2048]: n_set.append(n)
    unique sorted; filter n>=64 <=3072
    for n in n_unique:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120: break
       # parent interp
       if parent is not None:
          a=interpolate_sequence(parent,n); try_candidate(state,a,f'parent_n{n}')
       # if n small or time allow deterministic; For many n, deterministic heavy. Do for n in [512,768,1024] maybe.
       if n in (512,640,768,896,1024):
          cands=deterministic_candidates(n, seed+n)
          # limit maybe 30 random candidates uniform? all
          for a in cands: try_candidate(state,a,'cand')
       # For other n, just uniform and a few Dirichlet
       for alpha in [0.1,0.3,1,5]:
           a=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*alpha); try_candidate(state,a,'dir')

Need top list management function top-level to avoid closure:
`def add_to_top(top, score, seq, max_keep=5):` modifies list. It appends tuple (score, seq.copy()), then if len>max_keep remove max score. No lambda.
Implement:
```
def add_to_top(top, score, seq, max_keep=5):
    if not np.isfinite(score) or seq is None: return
    top.append((float(score), np.asarray(seq,float).copy()))
    if len(top) > max_keep:
       worst=0
       for i in range(1,len(top)):
          if top[i][0] > top[worst][0]: worst=i
       top.pop(worst)
```
Scores lower better; remove largest.

Let's write code carefully, ensuring no syntax errors.

Detailed code draft:

```
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import linprog
from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix

def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()
```
Actually `1 << (m-1).bit_length()` for m int. If m=3, (2).bit_length=2 ->4.

`lin_convolve`:
```
def lin_convolve(u, v):
    u=np.asarray(u, dtype=np.float64); v=np.asarray(v,dtype=np.float64)
    n=len(u); m=len(v)
    if n==0 or m==0: return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    size=n+m-1
    fft_size=next_pow2(size)
    U=np.fft.rfft(u, fft_size)
    V=np.fft.rfft(v, fft_size)
    out=np.fft.irfft(U*V, fft_size)[:size]
    out=np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    return out
```
If values huge? normalized small.

`scale`? okay.

`fast_conv_same(a)`: use `lin_convolve(a,a)` but for same we can use A*A (slightly faster):
```
def fast_conv_same(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=len(a); size=2*n-1; fft_size=next_pow2(size)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_size)
    out=np.fft.irfft(A*A, fft_size)[:size]
    return np.maximum(out,0.0)
```

`score_direct(a)`:
```
def score_direct(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n=len(a)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s<=1e-300: return float('inf')
    an=a/s
    M=float(fast_conv_same(an).max())
    return float(2.0*n*M)
```

`normalize_nonneg(a, floor=0.0)`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a, floor=0.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim !=1: a=a.ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    if floor > 0.0:
        a=np.maximum(a, floor)
    else:
        a=np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        l=len(a)
        return np.ones(l, dtype=np.float64)/l if l>0 else a
    return a/s
```
If floor=1e-12, and a all zeros, sum= n*1e-12, normalized uniform. Good.

`softmax`:
```
def softmax(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    if x.size==0: return x
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
       x=np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, -np.inf)
    mx=np.max(x)
    if not np.isfinite(mx): return np.full(x.shape,1.0/x.size)
    y=x-mx
    e=np.exp(y)
    s=float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-300:
       return np.full(x.shape,1.0/x.size)
    return e/s
```

`soft_active_weights`:
```
def soft_active_weights(conv, tau):
    conv=np.asarray(conv,float)
    if conv.size==0: return conv
    if tau is None or tau <= 1e-300:
       w=np.zeros_like(conv); w[int(np.argmax(conv))]=1.0; return w
    z=(conv - float(conv.max()))/tau
    z = z - float(np.max(z)) # should 0
    e=np.exp(z)
    s=float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-300:
       w=np.zeros_like(conv); w[int(np.argmax(conv))]=1.0; return w
    return e/s
```
If tau very small, z for max=0, others -inf? exp ok.

`weighted_corr(lam, f)` as above.

`blend_line_search`:
```
def blend_line_search(f, g, curr_M, alphas=None):
    if alphas is None: alphas=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0)
    best_a=f.copy(); best_M=float(curr_M); best_conv=None
    # include convex combo maybe g only? alphas includes 1
    for alpha in alphas:
       if alpha <=0: continue
       a=(1.0-alpha)*f + alpha*g
       a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
       s=float(a.sum())
       if s<=1e-300: continue
       a=a/s
       conv=fast_conv_same(a)
       M=float(conv.max())
       if M < best_M - 1e-15:
          best_M=M; best_a=a.copy(); best_conv=conv
    if best_conv is None:
       conv=fast_conv_same(best_a); best_conv=conv
       best_M=float(conv.max())
    return best_a, best_M, best_conv
```
If no improvement, returns f and conv recomputed.

`interpolate_sequence`:
```
def interpolate_sequence(parent, new_n):
    parent=np.asarray(parent,float)
    if len(parent)==new_n: return parent.copy()
    if len(parent)==1: return np.full(new_n, float(parent[0]))
    xo=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,len(parent))
    xn=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n)
    return np.interp(xn,xo,parent)
```

`perturb_simplex`:
```
def perturb_simplex(base, scale, seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    base=np.asarray(base,float)
    v=base + rng.standard_normal(len(base))*scale
    return normalize_nonneg(v,0.0)
```

`append_normalized` and `add_to_top` top-level.

`append_normalized(c,v)`:
```
def append_normalized(c,v):
    v=np.asarray(v,float)
    v=np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    v=np.maximum(v,0.0)
    s=float(v.sum())
    if s>1e-300:
       c.append(v/s)
    return c
```

`deterministic_candidates` use `append_normalized`. Ensure no nested.

```
def deterministic_candidates(n, seed=0):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    c=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    idx=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    append_normalized(c, np.ones(n))
    for p in (0.25,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0):
        append_normalized(c, (idx+1.0)**p)
        append_normalized(c, (n-idx)**p)
        append_normalized(c, (idx+1.0)**p + 0.5*(n-idx)**p)
    for p in (0.5,1.0,2.0):
        append_normalized(c, x**p)
        append_normalized(c, (1.0-x)**p)
        append_normalized(c, x**p + 0.5*(1.0-x)**p)
    for m in (1,2,3,5,7):
        for amp in (0.2,0.5,0.8,0.99):
            append_normalized(c, 1.0 + amp*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*m*x))
            append_normalized(c, 1.0 + amp*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*m*x))
            append_normalized(c, 1.0 + amp*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*m*x + 0.5))
    for alpha in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0,10.0):
        append_normalized(c, rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha)))
    for tr in range(6):
        k=int(rng.integers(1, 8))
        amp=0.3 + 0.5*float(rng.random())
        phase=float(rng.random()*6.28318)
        v=1.0 + amp*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*k*x + phase) + 0.3*amp*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*(k+1)*x + phase)
        append_normalized(c, v)
    d=np.minimum(x,1.0-x) + 1e-8
    for p in (0.5,1.0,2.0):
        append_normalized(c, 1.0/d**p)
    append_normalized(c, np.exp(-16.0*(x-0.5)*(x-0.5)))
    for period in (2,3,5,8):
        append_normalized(c, 1.0 + 0.5*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*idx/period))
        append_normalized(c, 1.0 + 0.4*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*idx/period))
    return c
```
Potential `n-idx` for idx float, last 0; power 0^p=0, ok positive elsewhere. `d` edge heavy high.

`try_candidate` needs call global `evaluate_sequence`. If not defined? It will be. But in helper top-level, if name missing? We can try/except NameError? It's global. Use:
```
try:
    actual = float(evaluate_sequence(an.tolist()))
except Exception:
    actual = direct
```

```
def try_candidate(state, a, label, force=False):
    if a is None: return float('inf')
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.ndim !=1 or a.size==0: return float('inf')
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s<=1e-15: return float('inf')
    an=a/s
    conv=fast_conv_same(an)
    M=float(conv.max())
    direct=float(2.0*an.size*M)
    if not np.isfinite(direct): return direct
    old_direct=state[3]
    if direct < state[3]:
        state[3]=direct
    do_eval = force or (state[0] is None) or (direct < old_direct - 1e-12)
    if do_eval:
        try:
            actual=float(evaluate_sequence(an.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            actual=direct
        if np.isfinite(actual) and (state[0] is None or actual < state[1]):
            state[0]=an.copy(); state[1]=float(actual); state[2]=int(an.size); state[3]=direct
            print(f"NEW BEST [{label}] actual={state[1]:.8f} direct={direct:.8f} n={state[2]}")
        elif state[0] is None and np.isfinite(direct):
            state[0]=an.copy(); state[1]=float(direct); state[2]=int(an.size); state[3]=direct
    return direct
```
If actual not finite and state[0] None, fallback direct.
If direct improved but actual not called? condition direct < old_direct - 1e-12, yes called. So state actual updates if actual lower.

One issue: If hidden actual evaluator has different scale (e.g., previous target 1.5 while direct 1.5), okay. If actual returns np.inf for some sequence due sum<0.01? normalized sum1 no.

`add_to_top`:
```
def add_to_top(top, score, seq, max_keep=5):
    if seq is None or not np.isfinite(score): return
    top.append((float(score), np.asarray(seq,float).copy()))
    if len(top) > max_keep:
        worst=0
        for i in range(1,len(top)):
            if top[i][0] > top[worst][0]: worst=i
        top.pop(worst)
```

`lse_mirror_optimize` as above. Need `verbose` print safe. Use no nested.

`game_sca_optimize`: Need `next_pow2` for size_l. For n=1, size_l next_pow2(1). In `Rt[L-1: L-1-n: -1]`, if n=1, stop=-1 works? Let's test Python: array length1, `arr[0:-1:-1]` returns [arr[0]]? Yes. If stop < -1? For n large, stop = L-1-n = (2n-2)-n = n-2, nonnegative until n=2 stop=0? For n=2, L=3, stop=0, `arr[2:0:-1]` indices 2,1 length2. Good. For n=1, stop=-1, okay.
Need ensure `h` length n. If due slice weird, maybe assert? Not in final. Could use explicit:
```
h = Rt[L-1-n+1 : L+1][::-1]  # indices from L-n to L? Wait L-1 down to L-n inclusive count n.
```
Let's derive safer: start stop indices. We want indices [L-1, L-2, ..., L-n]. In Python increasing slice from L-n to L: `Rt[L-n : L][::-1]`. Since L-n = (2n-1)-n = n-1. For n=3, indices 2..4 reverse ->4,3,2. Good. For n=1, L=1, L-n=0, L=1 -> arr[0:1][::-1] -> index0. This is simpler. Use `h = Rt[L-n : L][::-1]`. But Rt length is n+L-1 =3n-2. L=2n-1, L index =2n-1 < length? 3n-2, for n≥1, 2n-1 <=3n-2 yes. Indices L-n to L-1. For n=2, L=3, indices1..2 reverse ->2,1. Good. So `h = np.maximum(Rt[L-n:L][::-1],0)`.
Check earlier h1 index1 for n2; yes. Use this.

`game_sca_optimize` detailed code:
```
def game_sca_optimize(a_init, time_limit, temp_frac=0.2, inner_block=20, beta=0.25, seed=0, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a_init,1e-12)
    n=int(a.size); L=2*n-1
    conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max())
    best=a.copy(); best_M=M
    size_g=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    size_l=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    Af_g=np.fft.rfft(a,size_g); Af_l=np.fft.rfft(a,size_l)
    g=a.copy(); g_avg=a.copy()
    T=max(1e-12, temp_frac*M)
    lam=soft_active_weights(conv,T); lam_avg=lam.copy()
    t0=time.monotonic(); outer=0; no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic() - t0 < float(time_limit):
       for _ in range(inner_block):
          Gg=np.fft.rfft(g,size_g)
          fg=np.fft.irfft(Af_g*Gg,size_g)[:L]
          row=softmax(fg / T)
          lamr=lam[::-1]
          Ll=np.fft.rfft(lamr,size_l)
          Rt=np.fft.irfft(Af_l*Ll,size_l)
          h=np.maximum(Rt[L-n:L][::-1],0.0)
          col=softmax(-h / T)
          g=col
          lam=row
          lam_avg=(1.0-beta)*lam_avg + beta*row
          g_avg=(1.0-beta)*g_avg + beta*col
       g_use=normalize_nonneg(g_avg,0.0)
       newa,newM,newconv=blend_line_search(a,g_use,M)
       improved=False
       if newM < M - 1e-15:
          a=newa; M=newM; conv=newconv; best_M_local? ...
          improved=True
       else:
          # MW using h
          hmax=float(h.max())
          if hmax>1e-300:
             eta=0.2/hmax
             a_mw=normalize_nonneg(a*np.exp(-eta*h)+1e-12,0.0)
             newa2,newM2,newconv2=blend_line_search(a,a_mw,M)
             if newM2 < M - 1e-15:
                a=newa2; M=newM2; conv=newconv2; improved=True
       if improved:
          if M < best_M - 1e-15:
             best_M=M; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
             if verbose and outer%10==0: print(...)
          Af_g=np.fft.rfft(a,size_g); Af_l=np.fft.rfft(a,size_l)
          temp_frac=max(0.05, temp_frac*0.95)
          T=max(1e-12, temp_frac*M)
          # keep direction but maybe blend with a
          g=g_use.copy(); g_avg=g_use.copy()
          lam=soft_active_weights(conv,T); lam_avg=lam.copy()
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          temp_frac=min(2.0, temp_frac*1.1)
          T=max(1e-12, temp_frac*M)
          if no_improve > 30:
             scale=0.002 + 0.004*(1 + (no_improve//30) % 5)
             a=perturb_simplex(best, scale, seed+outer)
             conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max())
             if M < best_M:
                best_M=M; best=a.copy()
             Af_g=np.fft.rfft(a,size_g); Af_l=np.fft.rfft(a,size_l)
             temp_frac=0.2; T=max(1e-12,temp_frac*M)
             g=a.copy(); g_avg=a.copy(); lam=soft_active_weights(conv,T); lam_avg=lam.copy()
             no_improve=0
       outer +=1
    return best, best_M
```
Need `h` defined if inner_block >0; yes. If no inner? inner_block at least 1. Ensure `best_M_local` not used.
When improved but M not best (maybe a improved from current but not global best), we still update caches. Good.
If `M` becomes > best_M after perturb? best remains.
Potential `T` too small after temp_frac=0.05 and M small (1e-3) ->5e-5, okay.
`g_use` maybe same as a; blend no improvement; MW maybe.

`lse_mirror_optimize` uses `weighted_corr` not cached; each iteration two FFT? soft weights and conv. `weighted_corr` uses lin_convolve f*lam_rev. Could optimize but fine.

`sca_lp_optimize`: Need if `n*n > max_elems` return best. Also if time_limit <=0 return.
```
def sca_lp_optimize(a_init, time_limit, seed=0, max_elems=1800000, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a_init,1e-12)
    n=int(a.size)
    if n*n > max_elems: return a, score_direct(a)
    L=2*n-1
    row_idx,col_idx=build_conv_structure(n)
    t_rows=np.arange(L,dtype=np.int32); t_cols=np.arange(L,dtype=np.int32)
    conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max()); best=a.copy(); best_M=M
    t0=time.monotonic(); it=0; no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        g=None
        try:
            g=sca_lp_minmax_step(a,row_idx,col_idx,t_rows,t_cols)
        except Exception:
            g=None
        if g is None:
            # run fast lse for brief? break
            break
        g=normalize_nonneg(g,0.0)
        newa,newM,newconv=blend_line_search(a,g,M)
        improved=False
        if newM < M - 1e-15:
            a=newa; M=newM; conv=newconv; improved=True
        else:
            # try LSE step maybe
            p=soft_active_weights(conv, max(1e-12, 0.05*M))
            h=weighted_corr(p,a)
            hmax=float(h.max())
            if hmax>1e-300:
               eta=0.2/hmax
               a_mw=normalize_nonneg(a*np.exp(-eta*h)+1e-12,0.0)
               newa2,newM2,newconv2=blend_line_search(a,a_mw,M)
               if newM2 < M - 1e-15:
                  a=newa2; M=newM2; conv=newconv2; improved=True
        if improved:
            if M < best_M - 1e-15:
                best_M=M; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
                if verbose and it%5==0: print(...)
        else:
            no_improve +=1
            if no_improve > 8:
                scale=0.002 + 0.003*(1+(no_improve//8)%4)
                a=perturb_simplex(best,scale,seed+it)
                conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max())
                if M < best_M: best_M=M; best=a.copy()
                no_improve=0
        it +=1
    return best,best_M
```
LP step uses same `sca_lp_minmax_step`. If LP solve fails often, break.
Need check time before LP: if remaining < 10 maybe skip.

`peak_shave_optimize` optional. Let's implement to polish:
```
def peak_shave_optimize(a_init, time_limit, seed=0, frac=0.05):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a_init,1e-12); n=len(a)
    conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max()); best=a.copy(); best_M=M
    t0=...; it=0; no_improve=0
    while time...:
       conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=conv.max(); k=int(np.argmax(conv))
       js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
       contrib=np.zeros(n)
       # vectorize: j slice js..je, partner k-j from k-js down k-je
       jslice=np.arange(js,je+1)
       contrib[jslice]=2.0*a[jslice]*a[k-jslice]
       total=float(contrib.sum())
       if total<=1e-300: break
       move=np.zeros(n)
       frac_cur=frac*(1+0.1*(it%5))
       move[jslice] = -0.5*frac_cur*a[jslice]*(contrib[jslice]/total)
       outside = np.concatenate((np.arange(0,js), np.arange(je+1,n))) if js>0 or je+1<n else np.nonzero(contrib<=total*0.1)[0]
       if outside.size>0:
          total_move=-float(move.sum())
          if total_move>0:
             vals=a[outside]
             svals=float(vals.sum())
             if svals>1e-300:
                move[outside] = total_move*vals/svals
             else:
                move[outside] = total_move/outside.size
       a_new=normalize_nonneg(a+move,0.0)
       newa,newM,newconv=blend_line_search(a,a_new,M)
       if newM < M -1e-15: update, no_improve=0
       else no_improve++; if no_improve>20: perturb
       it+=1
    return best,best_M
```
Vectorize `a[k-jslice]`: k-jslice array descending, okay. For js=0, first concat empty. `outside` may be empty if peak spans whole sequence (js=0,je=n-1). Then fallback to low contrib inside? We need move from high contrib to low contrib inside same span to reduce peak? If peak spans whole (k near center for n small?), can redistribute within span: subtract high contrib, add low contrib. Implement if outside empty:
```
if outside.size==0:
   # choose inside positions with below average contrib
   avg=total/(je-js+1)
   inside=np.arange(js,je+1)
   low=inside[contrib[inside] < avg]
   high=inside[contrib[inside] >= avg]
   if low.size and high.size:
      move[high] = -0.5*frac_cur*a[high]*(contrib[high]/total)
      s=a[low].sum(); if s>0: move[low]= -move.sum()*a[low]/s
```
Maybe too much. We can skip peak_shave if outside empty. Not critical.

We'll include a simpler version.

Now, propose_candidate final code with top list. Need local variable `top=[]`.
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    budget=max(1.0,float(budget_s))
    deadline=t_start + budget - 25.0
    state=[None,float('inf'),0,float('inf')]
    top=[]
    # parent
    parent=None
    try:
       p=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
       if p.ndim==1 and p.size>0:
          p=np.where(np.isfinite(p),p,0.0); p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
          if p.sum()>1e-10:
             parent=normalize_nonneg(p,0.0)
             d=try_candidate(state,parent,'parent',force=True)
             add_to_top(top,d,parent)
    except Exception:
       pass
    if parent is None:
       parent=None
    # candidate n list
    n_list=[]
    if parent is not None: n_list.append(int(parent.size))
    for n in (256,384,512,640,768,896,1024,1280,1536,2048): n_list.append(n)
    # unique sorted
    unique=[]
    for n in n_list:
       if n>=64 and n<=3072 and n not in unique: unique.append(n)
    unique.sort()
    for n in unique:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 100: break
       an = interpolate_sequence(parent,n) if parent is not None else np.ones(n)
       an = normalize_nonneg(an,0.0)
       d=try_candidate(state,an,f'parent_n{n}')
       add_to_top(top,d,an)
       if n in (512,640,768,896,1024):
          cands=deterministic_candidates(n, seed+n)
          # To limit, maybe sample up to 60 if too many
          if len(cands)>80:
             # shuffle deterministic? use rng
             rng2=np.random.default_rng(seed+n)
             # cannot shuffle list of arrays? We can take first 80? deterministic order okay.
             cands=cands[:80]
          for idx,a in enumerate(cands):
             dd=try_candidate(state,a,f'cand_n{n}_{idx}')
             add_to_top(top,dd,a)
             if time.monotonic() > deadline - 100: break
       else:
          # a few random
          for alpha in (0.1,0.3,1.0,5.0):
             try a=rng_dirichlet? Need rng. Use np.random.default_rng(seed+n+alpha)
             rng2=np.random.default_rng(seed+n*10+int(alpha*100))
             a=rng2.dirichlet(np.full(n, alpha))
             dd=try_candidate(state,a,'dir')
             add_to_top(top,dd,a)
    # if no best -> uniform
    if state[0] is None:
       a=np.ones(768)/768
       d=try_candidate(state,a,'uniform',force=True); add_to_top(top,d,a)
    # symmetrize top arrays
    for sc,a in list(top):
       s=(a+a[::-1])/2.0; s=normalize_nonneg(s,0.0)
       d=try_candidate(state,s,'sym')
       add_to_top(top,d,s)
    # blend top arrays
    top_copy=list(top)
    for i in range(len(top_copy)):
      for j in range(i+1,len(top_copy)):
        a1=top_copy[i][1]; a2=top_copy[j][1]
        for alpha in (0.25,0.5,0.75):
           b=alpha*a1+(1-alpha)*a2; b=normalize_nonneg(b,0.0)
           d=try_candidate(state,b,'blend')
           add_to_top(top,d,b)
    best_a=state[0].copy() if state[0] is not None else np.ones(768)/768
    # lse
    rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
    if rem > 100:
       t=min(120.0, rem-250.0)
       if t>10:
          res_a,res_M=lse_mirror_optimize(best_a,t,tau_frac=0.05,move_scale=0.3,seed=seed,verbose=True)
          d=try_candidate(state,res_a,'lse1',force=True); add_to_top(top,d,res_a)
          if state[0] is not None: best_a=state[0].copy()
    # game main
    rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
    if rem > 400:
       t=min(650.0, rem-250.0)
       if t>30:
          res_a,res_M=game_sca_optimize(best_a,t,temp_frac=0.2,inner_block=20,beta=0.25,seed=seed+10,verbose=True)
          d=try_candidate(state,res_a,'game1',force=True); add_to_top(top,d,res_a)
          best_a=state[0].copy() if state[0] is not None else best_a
    # game on second if time
    rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
    if rem > 250 and len(top)>1:
       # choose second distinct from best? top[1]
       sec_a=top[1][1]
       # maybe interpolate to best length if length differs? We can optimize as is.
       t=min(180.0, rem-150.0)
       if t>20:
          res_a,res_M=game_sca_optimize(sec_a,t,temp_frac=0.25,inner_block=20,beta=0.25,seed=seed+20,verbose=False)
          d=try_candidate(state,res_a,'game2',force=True); add_to_top(top,d,res_a)
          best_a=state[0].copy() if state[0] is not None else best_a
    # LP polish
    rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
    if rem > 150:
       t=min(180.0, rem-80.0)
       if t>20:
          res_a,res_M=sca_lp_optimize(best_a,t,seed=seed+30,verbose=True)
          d=try_candidate(state,res_a,'lp1',force=True); add_to_top(top,d,res_a)
          best_a=state[0].copy() if state[0] is not None else best_a
    # final lse polish
    rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
    if rem > 80:
       t=min(80.0, rem-30.0)
       if t>10:
          res_a,res_M=lse_mirror_optimize(best_a,t,tau_frac=0.02,move_scale=0.15,seed=seed+40,verbose=False)
          d=try_candidate(state,res_a,'lse2',force=True); add_to_top(top,d,res_a)
          best_a=state[0].copy()
    # peak shave
    rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
    if rem > 50:
       t=min(40.0, rem-20.0)
       if t>5:
          res_a,res_M=peak_shave_optimize(best_a,t,seed=seed+50,verbose=False)
          d=try_candidate(state,res_a,'peak',force=True); add_to_top(top,d,res_a)
          best_a=state[0].copy()
    # random restarts/hopping
    rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
    restart=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 15 and rem > 0:
       rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
       if rem < 12: break
       scale = 0.004 + 0.008*float(restart % 5)
       a=perturb_simplex(best_a, scale, seed+1000+restart)
       t=min(10.0, rem-5.0)
       if t < 5: break
       res_a,res_M=lse_mirror_optimize(a,t,tau_frac=0.05,move_scale=0.25,seed=seed+2000+restart,verbose=False)
       d=try_candidate(state,res_a,f'restart{restart}',force=False); add_to_top(top,d,res_a)
       if state[0] is not None: best_a=state[0].copy()
       restart +=1
       if restart >= 30: break
    # final blends after optimizers
    top_copy=list(top)
    for i in range(len(top_copy)):
      for j in range(i+1,len(top_copy)):
        a1=top_copy[i][1]; a2=top_copy[j][1]
        for alpha in (0.5,):
           b=alpha*a1+(1-alpha)*a2; b=normalize_nonneg(b,0.0)
           d=try_candidate(state,b,'final_blend',force=False)
           add_to_top(top,d,b)
    # final evaluate
    if state[0] is not None:
       try:
          actual=float(evaluate_sequence(state[0].tolist()))
       except Exception:
          actual=state[1]
       if np.isfinite(actual) and actual < state[1]:
          state[1]=actual
       print(f"FINAL score={state[1]:.8f} n={state[2]}")
       out=[float(x) for x in state[0]]
    else:
       out=[1.0/768]*768
    return out
```

```
def peak_shave_optimize(a_init, time_limit, seed=0, frac=0.05, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a_init,1e-12)
    n=int(a.size)
    conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max()); best=a.copy(); best_M=M
    t0=time.monotonic(); it=0; no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max())
       if M < best_M: best_M=M; best=a.copy()
       k=int(np.argmax(conv))
       js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(n-1,k)
       idx=np.arange(js,je+1,dtype=np.int64)
       contrib=np.zeros(n)
       if idx.size>0:
           partner=k-idx
           contrib[idx]=2.0*a[idx]*a[partner]
       total=float(contrib.sum())
       if total <= 1e-300:
           break
       move=np.zeros(n)
       frac_cur=frac*(1.0 + 0.05*(it % 5))
       move[idx] = -0.5*frac_cur*a[idx]*(contrib[idx]/total)
       outside = np.concatenate((np.arange(0,js,dtype=np.int64), np.arange(je+1,n,dtype=np.int64)))
       if outside.size == 0:
           # fallback use low contributing inside
           avg=total/idx.size if idx.size>0 else 0.0
           low=idx[contrib[idx] < avg]
           if low.size == 0:
               no_improve +=1
               it +=1
               if no_improve>20:
                  a=perturb_simplex(best, 0.003, seed+it); conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max()); no_improve=0
               continue
           svals=float(a[low].sum())
           if svals > 1e-300:
               move[low] = -float(move.sum())*a[low]/svals
           else:
               move[low] = -float(move.sum())/low.size
       else:
           total_move=-float(move.sum())
           if total_move > 0:
               vals=a[outside]
               svals=float(vals.sum())
               if svals > 1e-300:
                   move[outside]=total_move*vals/svals
               else:
                   move[outside]=total_move/outside.size
       a_new=normalize_nonneg(a+move,0.0)
       newa,newM,newconv=blend_line_search(a,a_new,M)
       if newM < M - 1e-15:
           a=newa; M=newM; conv=newconv; no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           if no_improve > 25:
               scale=0.002 + 0.003*(1+(no_improve//25)%4)
               a=perturb_simplex(best,scale,seed+it)
               conv=fast_conv_same(a); M=float(conv.max())
               no_improve=0
       it +=1
    if M < best_M:
       best_M=M; best=a.copy()
    return best, best_M
```

def next_pow2(m):
    m=int(m)
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```

If actual inf and direct finite, fallback direct update. But condition `elif state[0] is None and np.isfinite(direct):` inside if do_eval; yes.
